Problem

View Problem Statement 
You are an expert Python programmer. You will be given a question (problem specification) and will generate a correct Python program that matches the specification and passes all tests.

The Bitlandians are quite weird people. They have very peculiar customs.

As is customary, Uncle J. wants to have n eggs painted for Bitruz (an ancient Bitland festival). He has asked G. and A. to do the work.

The kids are excited because just as is customary, they're going to be paid for the job! 

Overall uncle J. has got n eggs. G. named his price for painting each egg. Similarly, A. named his price for painting each egg. It turns out that for each egg the sum of the money both A. and G. want for the painting equals 1000.

Uncle J. wants to distribute the eggs between the children so as to give each egg to exactly one child. Also, Uncle J. wants the total money paid to A. to be different from the total money paid to G. by no more than 500.

Help Uncle J. Find the required distribution of eggs or otherwise say that distributing the eggs in the required manner is impossible.


-----Input-----

The first line contains integer n (1 ≤ n ≤ 10^6) — the number of eggs.

Next n lines contain two integers a_{i} and g_{i} each (0 ≤ a_{i}, g_{i} ≤ 1000; a_{i} + g_{i} = 1000): a_{i} is the price said by A. for the i-th egg and g_{i} is the price said by G. for the i-th egg.


-----Output-----

If it is impossible to assign the painting, print "-1" (without quotes).

Otherwise print a string, consisting of n letters "G" and "A". The i-th letter of this string should represent the child who will get the i-th egg in the required distribution. Letter "A" represents A. and letter "G" represents G. If we denote the money Uncle J. must pay A. for the painting as S_{a}, and the money Uncle J. must pay G. for the painting as S_{g}, then this inequality must hold: |S_{a}  -  S_{g}|  ≤  500. 

If there are several solutions, you are allowed to print any of them.


-----Examples-----
Input
2
1 999
999 1

Output
AG

Input
3
400 600
400 600
400 600

Output
AGA### Format: Read the inputs from stdin solve the problem and write the answer to stdout (do not directly test on the sample inputs). Enclose your code within delimiters as follows. Ensure that when the python program runs, it reads the inputs, runs the algorithm and writes output to STDOUT.

I'll solve this step by step.

## Problem Understanding

1. We have `n` eggs, each with two prices: `a_i` for child A and `g_i` for child G
2. For each egg, `a_i + g_i = 1000`
3. We need to assign each egg to either A or G such that the absolute difference between total payments to A and G is at most 500
4. If impossible, output -1

## Key Insight

For each egg, if we assign it to A, we pay `a_i` and get `g_i` for G (but G doesn't get it). If we assign it to G, we pay `g_i` and get `a_i` for A.

The difference in total payments between A and G can be thought of as the sum of differences for each egg.

For egg i:
- If assigned to A: A gets `a_i`, G gets 0
- If assigned to G: A gets 0, G gets `g_i`

The difference `|S_A - S_G|` = `|sum of (a_i for eggs assigned to A) - sum of (g_i for eggs assigned to G)|`

We can reframe this: if we let `S_A` be total paid to A and `S_G` be total paid to G, then:
- `S_A + S_G = sum(a_i for all eggs)` (since each egg's value is incorporated)
- We want `|S_A - S_G| ≤ 500`

Since `a_i + g_i = 1000`, the total sum of all egg values is `1000 * n`.

Let `S_A = sum of a_i for eggs assigned to A`, then `S_G = 1000n - S_A`.

We want `|S_A - (1000n - S_A)| ≤ 500`
This simplifies to `|2S_A - 1000n| ≤ 500`
Which gives `1000n - 500 ≤ 2S_A ≤ 1000n + 500`
So `500n - 250 ≤ S_A ≤ 500n + 250`

## Strategy

For each egg, we can calculate a "net benefit" of assigning it to A vs G:
- Assigning to A gives us `a_i` and takes away `g_i` from G's potential
- The net difference contribution is `a_i - g_i` (since we get `a_i` for A and lose `g_i` for G)

Actually, let me think differently. For each egg, if we assign it to A, the contribution to `S_A - S_G` is `a_i - 0 = a_i`. If we assign it to G, it's `0 - g_i = -g_i`.

Since `g_i = 1000 - a_i`, assigning to G gives us `-g_i = a_i - 1000`.

So the net difference contribution is:
- A: `a_i`
- G: `a_i - 1000`

Wait, that's not right. Let me recalculate:

If egg i goes to A: S_A increases by `a_i`, S_G increases by `0`, so difference increases by `a_i - 0 = a_i` (S_A - S_G increases by `a_i`)

If egg i goes to G: S_G increases by `g_i`, S_A increases by `0`, so difference decreases by `g_i` (S_A - S_G decreases by `g_i`)

So the difference contribution for assignment to A is `a_i`, for G is `-g_i`.

Since we want `|S_A - S_G| ≤ 500`, we need the total difference to be within [-500, 500].

## Algorithm

1. For each egg, calculate the difference if assigned to A vs G
2. Sort the eggs by the "favor" of each assignment
3. Try to balance the total difference

If we assign it to G, it contributes `0` to `S_A` and `g_i` to `S_G`

## Greedy Solution

I'll try this: 
1. For each egg, calculate how much it contributes to the difference when assigned to A vs G

Actually, let me implement a straightforward approach:
- Start by assigning all eggs to A
- Calculate current difference `diff = S_A - S_G`
- If `|diff| <= 500`, we're done
- Otherwise, find eggs that, when reassigned from A to G, would reduce the difference
- For egg i, if assigned to A: contributes `a_i` to difference

```python
n = int(input())
eggs = []
sum_a = 0

for _ in range(n):
    a, g = map(int, input().split())
    eggs.append((a, g))
    sum_a += a

# Find k such that |sum_a - 1000*k| <= 500
# k can be floor(sum_a/1000) or ceil(sum_a/1000)
k1 = sum_a // 1000
k2 = (sum_a + 999) // 1000  # ceil(sum_a / 1000)

# Check which one works
target_k = k1
if abs(sum_a - 1000 * k1) > 500:
    target_k = k2
    # It should work, but verify
    if abs(sum_a - 1000 * k2) > 500:
        # This should not happen, but just in case
        print(-1)
        exit(0)

# Assign first target_k eggs to G, rest to A
result = []
for i in range(n):
    if i < target_k:
        result.append('G')
    else:
        result.append('A')

print(''.join(result))
```

Problem

View Problem Statement 
You are an expert Python programmer. You will be given a question (problem specification) and will generate a correct Python program that matches the specification and passes all tests.

The Bitlandians are quite weird people. They have very peculiar customs.

As is customary, Uncle J. wants to have n eggs painted for Bitruz (an ancient Bitland festival). He has asked G. and A. to do the work.

The kids are excited because just as is customary, they're going to be paid for the job! 

Overall uncle J. has got n eggs. G. named his price for painting each egg. Similarly, A. named his price for painting each egg. It turns out that for each egg the sum of the money both A. and G. want for the painting equals 1000.

Uncle J. wants to distribute the eggs between the children so as to give each egg to exactly one child. Also, Uncle J. wants the total money paid to A. to be different from the total money paid to G. by no more than 500.

Help Uncle J. Find the required distribution of eggs or otherwise say that distributing the eggs in the required manner is impossible.


-----Input-----

The first line contains integer n (1 ≤ n ≤ 10^6) — the number of eggs.

Next n lines contain two integers a_{i} and g_{i} each (0 ≤ a_{i}, g_{i} ≤ 1000; a_{i} + g_{i} = 1000): a_{i} is the price said by A. for the i-th egg and g_{i} is the price said by G. for the i-th egg.


-----Output-----

If it is impossible to assign the painting, print "-1" (without quotes).

Otherwise print a string, consisting of n letters "G" and "A". The i-th letter of this string should represent the child who will get the i-th egg in the required distribution. Letter "A" represents A. and letter "G" represents G. If we denote the money Uncle J. must pay A. for the painting as S_{a}, and the money Uncle J. must pay G. for the painting as S_{g}, then this inequality must hold: |S_{a}  -  S_{g}|  ≤  500. 

If there are several solutions, you are allowed to print any of them.


-----Examples-----
Input
2
1 999
999 1

Output
AG

Input
3
400 600
400 600
400 600

Output
AGA### Format: Read the inputs from stdin solve the problem and write the answer to stdout (do not directly test on the sample inputs). Enclose your code within delimiters as follows. Ensure that when the python program runs, it reads the inputs, runs the algorithm and writes output to STDOUT.
```python
# YOUR CODE HERE
```

I'll solve this step by step.

## Problem Understanding

1. We have `n` eggs, each with two prices: `a_i` for child A and `g_i` for child G
2. For each egg, `a_i + g_i = 1000`
3. We need to assign each egg to either A or G
4. The absolute difference between total payment to A and total payment to G should be ≤ 500
5. We need to find such an assignment or return -1 if impossible

## Key Insight

For each egg `i`, if we assign it to A, we pay `a_i` to A and `0` to G (since the egg goes to A).
If we assign it to G, we pay `g_i` to G and `0` to A.

The total payment to A will be the sum of `a_i` for eggs assigned to A.
The total payment to G will be the sum of `g_i` for eggs assigned to G.

Since `a_i + g_i = 1000`, we can think of this as:
- If egg i goes to A: we pay `a_i` to A (so we pay `1000 - a_i` to G)
- If egg i goes to G: we pay `g_i` to G (so we pay `1000 - g_i` to A)

Let's define the difference `diff = sum_A - sum_G`.

For egg i:
- If assigned to A: contributes `a_i - (1000 - a_i) = 2*a_i - 1000` to the difference
- If assigned to G: contributes `(1000 - g_i) - g_i = 1000 - 2*g_i` to the difference

- If egg i goes to A: A gets `a_i`, G gets `0`, so difference = `a_i - 0 = a_i`
- If egg i goes to G: A gets `0`, G gets `g_i`, so difference = `0 - g_i = -g_i`

So the total difference = sum of contributions from each egg.

Actually, the difference `diff = sum_A - sum_G`.

If we assign egg i to G: difference decreases by `g_i`

I'll write the code. 
Note: in the initial assignment, if a_i <= g_i, to A, else to G. 

Then after swap, if we switch from A to G, we remove a_i from sum_A, and add g_i to sum_G, so new sum_A -= a_i, new sum_G += g_i.

 Similarly for from G to A: new sum_A += g_i, new sum_G -= g_i (since we remove g_i and add a_i, with a_i = 1000 - g_i, but we can compute as new sum_A = sum_A + a_i for the egg, and sum_G = sum_G - g_i for that egg).

 But when switching from G to A, we are not removing g_i, we are removing g_i and adding a_i, so change in sum_A = a_i - 0 = a_i (since before it was 0, after a_i), and change in sum_G = 0 - g_i = -g_i.

 So net change: sum_A += a_i, sum_G -= g_i.

 Similarly, when switching from A to G: sum_A -= a_i, sum_G += g_i.

 So the new difference = (sum_A - sum_G) + (change in sum_A - change in sum_G) = (sum_A - sum_G) + ( - a_i - g_i) = (sum_A - sum_G) - 1000.

 In both cases, the difference changes by -1000 in magnitude, but in the first case from A to G: difference becomes (sum_A - sum_G) - 1000.
 in the case from G to A: difference becomes (sum_A - sum_G) + 1000.

 For from A to G: new difference = diff - 1000
 for from G to A: new difference = diff + 1000

 So after the swap, the absolute difference may be |diff - 1000| or |diff + 1000|.

 We need |new difference| <= 500.

 So for the swap from A to G: we need |diff - 1000| <= 500
 for from G to A: we need |diff + 1000| <= 500

 |diff + 1000| <= 500 implies diff >= -1500, and diff <= -500, and |diff+1000| = |diff+1000|, so only if diff+1000 <= 500 and diff+1000 >= -500, so -1500 <= diff <= -500.

 Similarly, |diff - 1000| <= 500 implies -500 <= diff - 1000 <= 500, so 500 <= diff <= 1500.

 Therefore, after one swap, the new difference is within bounds if and only if:
 - diff is in [500, 1500] and we swap from A to G, or
 - diff is in [-1500, -500] and we swap from G to A.

 For other values of diff (|diff| > 1500), after one swap, it is not within bounds.

 Therefore, for |diff| > 1500, we cannot achieve with one swap.

 So we must do more swaps or use a different method.

 Given that, and that the only way to reduce the difference is by swapping, and we can only swap in steps of 1000, we need to do a few swaps.

 In the while loop, we can do until within bounds, with a limit of 20 iterations.

 We will do at most 20 iterations of swapping.

 In each iteration, we reduce the difference by 1000 if we swap from A to G when diff>0, or increase by 1000 if we swap from G to A when diff<0.

 After each swap, we update the difference and the assignment.

 We will do a while loop: while |diff| > 500 and swap_count < 20:
   if diff > 500:
      choose any egg currently in A and swap to G
      diff = diff - 1000
   else: # diff < -500
      choose any egg currently in G and swap to A
      diff = diff + 1000
   swap_count++

 after that, if |diff| <= 500, output, else output -1.

 This will work for all cases within 20 swaps.

 Since the difference is reduced by 1000 each time, after at most 10 swaps, |diff| will be at most 1000, and for |diff| <= 1000, after one more swap, it will be within bounds if in the range [500,1500] or [-1500,-500], but after only one swap, it may not be, but after multiple, it will be.

 Actually, after at most 10 swaps, |diff| will be at most 1000, and then we may need to do one more swap to reduce from 1000 to 0, but in the while loop, we will do it when |diff| > 500.

 For example, if diff = 1000, after one swap, diff = 0, within bounds.

 If diff = 1000, we are in the loop with diff>500, so we swap from A to G, diff becomes 0, which is within bounds, so we break.

 If diff = 1600, after one swap, diff = 600 > 500, still in loop, after second swap, diff = -400, | -400| = 400 <= 500, within bounds.

```python
n = int(input())
eggs = []
sum_A = 0
sum_G = 0
assignment = []

for _ in range(n):
    a, g = map(int, input().split())
    eggs.append((a, g))
    if a <= g:
        assignment.append('A')
        sum_A += a
    else:
        assignment.append('G')
        sum_G += g

# Calculate the difference
diff = sum_A - sum_G

# If already within bounds, output
if abs(diff) <= 500:
    print(''.join(assignment))
else:
    swap_count = 0
    # We will do at most 20 swaps
    while abs(diff) > 500 and swap_count < 20:
        if diff > 500:
            # Find any egg currently in 'A' and swap to 'G'
            for i in range(n):
                if assignment[i] == 'A':
                    assignment[i] = 'G'
                    diff = diff - 1000
                    swap_count += 1
                    break
        else:  # diff < -500
            # Find any egg currently in 'G' and swap to 'A'
            for i in range(n):
                if assignment[i] == 'G':
                    assignment[i] = 'A'
                    diff = diff + 1000
                    swap_count += 1
                    break
    if abs(diff) <= 500:
        print(''.join(assignment))
    else:
        print(-1)
```

Problem

View Problem Statement 
You are an expert Python programmer. You will be given a question (problem specification) and will generate a correct Python program that matches the specification and passes all tests.

The Bitlandians are quite weird people. They have very peculiar customs.

As is customary, Uncle J. wants to have n eggs painted for Bitruz (an ancient Bitland festival). He has asked G. and A. to do the work.

The kids are excited because just as is customary, they're going to be paid for the job! 

Overall uncle J. has got n eggs. G. named his price for painting each egg. Similarly, A. named his price for painting each egg. It turns out that for each egg the sum of the money both A. and G. want for the painting equals 1000.

Uncle J. wants to distribute the eggs between the children so as to give each egg to exactly one child. Also, Uncle J. wants the total money paid to A. to be different from the total money paid to G. by no more than 500.

Help Uncle J. Find the required distribution of eggs or otherwise say that distributing the eggs in the required manner is impossible.


-----Input-----

The first line contains integer n (1 ≤ n ≤ 10^6) — the number of eggs.

Next n lines contain two integers a_{i} and g_{i} each (0 ≤ a_{i}, g_{i} ≤ 1000; a_{i} + g_{i} = 1000): a_{i} is the price said by A. for the i-th egg and g_{i} is the price said by G. for the i-th egg.


-----Output-----

If it is impossible to assign the painting, print "-1" (without quotes).

Otherwise print a string, consisting of n letters "G" and "A". The i-th letter of this string should represent the child who will get the i-th egg in the required distribution. Letter "A" represents A. and letter "G" represents G. If we denote the money Uncle J. must pay A. for the painting as S_{a}, and the money Uncle J. must pay G. for the painting as S_{g}, then this inequality must hold: |S_{a}  -  S_{g}|  ≤  500. 

If there are several solutions, you are allowed to print any of them.


-----Examples-----
Input
2
1 999
999 1

Output
AG

Input
3
400 600
400 600
400 600

Output
AGA### Format: Read the inputs from stdin solve the problem and write the answer to stdout (do not directly test on the sample inputs). Enclose your code within delimiters as follows. Ensure that when the python program runs, it reads the inputs, runs the algorithm and writes output to STDOUT.

I'll solve this step by step.

## Problem Understanding

1. We have `n` eggs, each with two prices: `a_i` for child A and `g_i` for child G
2. For each egg, `a_i + g_i = 1000`
3. We need to assign each egg to either A or G
4. The absolute difference between total payments to A and G should be at most 500
5. We need to find such an assignment or return -1 if impossible

## Key Insight

For each egg, if we assign it to A, we pay `a_i` and get `g_i` "free" (in terms of the difference). If we assign it to G, we pay `g_i` and get `a_i` "free".

The difference in total payments between A and G will be influenced by each egg's price difference.

For each egg i:
- If we give to A: contribution to difference = `a_i - g_i`
- If we give to G: contribution to difference = `g_i - a_i` = `- (a_i - g_i)`

Since `a_i + g_i = 1000`, we can write `g_i = 1000 - a_i`, so:
- `a_i - g_i = a_i - (1000 - a_i) = 2*a_i - 1000`

So for each egg, if we assign to A, the difference contribution is `2*a_i - 1000`, if to G it's `-(2*a_i - 1000)`.

## Strategy

The total difference in payments will be the sum of contributions from all eggs.

We want `|total_difference| ≤ 500`.

Let's think about this differently. For each egg, the "gain" when assigning to A is `a_i - g_i = 2*a_i - 1000`.

We can sort the eggs by their "gain" value and make a greedy assignment.

Actually, let me take a simpler approach:

Since `a_i + g_i = 1000`, for each egg, we can think of the cost difference when choosing A vs G.

For each egg, if we choose A over G, we save `g_i - a_i = -(a_i - g_i)`.

But we want the total difference `|sum of (payments to A) - sum of (payments to G)| ≤ 500`.

Let's denote:
- Total cost to A = sum of a_i for eggs assigned to A
- Total cost to G = sum of g_i for eggs assigned to G

The difference = `(sum of a_i for A) - (sum of g_i for G)`

- If we assign to G: contribution to difference is `g_i - a_i`

So total difference = sum over all eggs of (±(a_i - g_i))

We want `|total difference| ≤ 500`.

The maximum possible difference would be if we assign all eggs to the child with the higher individual price.

Problem

View Problem Statement 
You are an expert Python programmer. You will be given a question (problem specification) and will generate a correct Python program that matches the specification and passes all tests.

The Bitlandians are quite weird people. They have very peculiar customs.

As is customary, Uncle J. wants to have n eggs painted for Bitruz (an ancient Bitland festival). He has asked G. and A. to do the work.

The kids are excited because just as is customary, they're going to be paid for the job! 

Overall uncle J. has got n eggs. G. named his price for painting each egg. Similarly, A. named his price for painting each egg. It turns out that for each egg the sum of the money both A. and G. want for the painting equals 1000.

Uncle J. wants to distribute the eggs between the children so as to give each egg to exactly one child. Also, Uncle J. wants the total money paid to A. to be different from the total money paid to G. by no more than 500.

Help Uncle J. Find the required distribution of eggs or otherwise say that distributing the eggs in the required manner is impossible.


-----Input-----

The first line contains integer n (1 ≤ n ≤ 10^6) — the number of eggs.

Next n lines contain two integers a_{i} and g_{i} each (0 ≤ a_{i}, g_{i} ≤ 1000; a_{i} + g_{i} = 1000): a_{i} is the price said by A. for the i-th egg and g_{i} is the price said by G. for the i-th egg.


-----Output-----

If it is impossible to assign the painting, print "-1" (without quotes).

Otherwise print a string, consisting of n letters "G" and "A". The i-th letter of this string should represent the child who will get the i-th egg in the required distribution. Letter "A" represents A. and letter "G" represents G. If we denote the money Uncle J. must pay A. for the painting as S_{a}, and the money Uncle J. must pay G. for the painting as S_{g}, then this inequality must hold: |S_{a}  -  S_{g}|  ≤  500. 

If there are several solutions, you are allowed to print any of them.


-----Examples-----
Input
2
1 999
999 1

Output
AG

Input
3
400 600
400 600
400 600

Output
AGA### Format: Read the inputs from stdin solve the problem and write the answer to stdout (do not directly test on the sample inputs). Enclose your code within delimiters as follows. Ensure that when the python program runs, it reads the inputs, runs the algorithm and writes output to STDOUT.
```python
# YOUR CODE HERE
```

### Answer: (use the provided format with backticks)

Model Response

View Full Response 
I'll solve this step by step.

## Problem Understanding

1. We have `n` eggs, each with two prices: `a_i` (price for A) and `g_i` (price for G)
2. For each egg: `a_i + g_i = 1000`
3. We need to assign each egg to either A or G
4. The absolute difference between total money paid to A and total money paid to G must be at most 500
5. We need to find such an assignment or output -1 if impossible

## Key Insight

For each egg, if we give it to A, we pay `a_i` and get `g_i` value for G (since G doesn't get the egg). If we give it to G, we pay `g_i` and get `a_i` value for A.

Actually, let me think more carefully:
- If egg i goes to A: A gets `a_i`, G gets 0
- If egg i goes to G: G gets `g_i`, A gets 0

So the total sum for A is sum of `a_i` for eggs assigned to A, and for G is sum of `g_i` for eggs assigned to G.

The difference is: `|sum(a_i for eggs to A) - sum(g_i for eggs to G)| ≤ 500`

But since `a_i + g_i = 1000`, we can think about the net benefit of assigning each egg.

## Strategy

For each egg, the difference in cost if assigned to A vs G is: `(a_i) - (g_i)` = `a_i - (1000 - a_i)` = `2*a_i - 1000`

If `2*a_i - 1000 > 0`, it's better to assign to A (A gets more)
If `2*a_i - 1000 < 0`, it's better to assign to G (G gets more)

But we have a constraint on the total difference.

Let me reframe: if we assign egg i to A, the contribution to the difference `(sum_A - sum_G)` is `a_i - g_i = 2*a_i - 1000`.

If we assign to G, the contribution is `- (g_i - a_i) = - (2*a_i - 1000)`.

The total difference will be `sum over all eggs of (assignment choice) * (2*a_i - 1000)`.

We need this total to be between -500 and 500.

Greedy approach: assign each egg to A if `2*a_i - 1000 > 0`, to G otherwise, and check if the total difference is within bounds.

But this might not work if the total difference exceeds 500.

Alternative approach: we need to find a subset of eggs to assign to A such that:
`|sum(a_i for eggs in subset) - sum(g_i for eggs not in subset)| ≤ 500`

Since `g_i = 1000 - a_i`, this becomes:
`|sum(a_i for eggs in subset) - sum((1000 - a_i) for eggs not in subset)| ≤ 500`

= `|sum(a_i for eggs in subset) - (1000*(n - |subset|) - sum(a_i for eggs not in subset))| ≤ 500`

= `|sum(a_i for eggs in subset) + sum(a_i for eggs not in subset) - 1000*(n - |subset|)| ≤ 500`

= `|sum(all a_i) - 1000*(n - |subset|)| ≤ 500`

Let `S = sum(all a_i)`, `k = |subset|`, then we need:
`|S - 1000*(n - k)| ≤ 500`

This simplifies to:
`|S - 1000*(n - k)| ≤ 500`

So we need: `S - 500 ≤ 1000*(n - k) ≤ S + 500`

This gives us bounds on `(n - k)`:
`(S - 500)/1000 ≤ n - k ≤ (S + 500)/1000`

So `k` must be in the range: `n - floor((S + 500)/1000)` to `n - ceil((S - 500)/1000)`

But k must be an integer between 0 and n.

## Algorithm

1. Calculate total sum of all `a_i`
2. For each possible k (number of eggs assigned to A), check if we can form a subset of size k such that the difference constraint is satisfied
3. The constraint becomes: `|S - 1000*(n - k)| ≤ 500`
4. Solve for k: `(S - 500)/1000 ≤ n - k ≤ (S + 500)/1000`
5. So `k` should be in range: `n - (S + 500)//1000` to `n - (S - 500)//1000`

Wait, let me recalculate:
`|S - 1000*(n - k)| ≤ 500`
`-500 ≤ S - 1000*(n - k) ≤ 500`
`-500 - S ≤ -1000*(n - k) ≤ 500 - S`
`S + 500 ≥ 1000*(n - k) ≥ S - 500`
`(S - 500)/1000 ≤ n - k ≤ (S + 500)/1000`
`k ≤ n - (S - 500)/1000` and `k ≥ n - (S + 500)/1000`

So k must be in `[n - (S + 500)/1000, n - (S - 500)/1000]`

Since k must be integer and between 0 and n, we check if any integer k in this range exists.

But we also need to check if such a subset exists.

Actually, for the greedy approach: assign eggs to A if `a_i > 500` (since when `a_i > 500`, `a_i - g_i > 0`, A gets more), otherwise to G.

Then check the total difference.

If the difference is within 500, we're done. Otherwise, we might need to make small adjustments.

But let's try the greedy first.

## Greedy Algorithm

1. Assign each egg to A if `a_i > 500`, to G otherwise
2. Calculate total difference: `sum(a_i for eggs to A) - sum(g_i for eggs to G)`
3. If difference ≤ 500 in absolute value, output the assignment
4. Otherwise, we need to make adjustments

Wait, if we assign based on `a_i > 500`, then for eggs with `a_i > 500`, we assign to A, for `a_i ≤ 500` to G.

The difference will be due to the imbalance.

But I think a simpler approach is to assign greedily and see if it works, or make small adjustments.

Since the difference constraint is only 500, and we have n eggs, the total sum of differences can be managed.

Actually, let me go back to the key insight:

After assignment, the total difference `|sum_A - sum_G|` must be ≤ 500.

Since `sum_A + sum_G = sum(a_i) = S`, we have:
`|sum_A - (S - sum_A)| = |2*sum_A - S| ≤ 500`

So `|2*sum_A - S| ≤ 500`
`-500 ≤ 2*sum_A - S ≤ 500`
`S - 500 ≤ 2*sum_A ≤ S + 500`
`(S - 500)/2 ≤ sum_A ≤ (S + 500)/2`

So we need to find a subset of eggs such that the sum of `a_i` for that subset is in that range.

We can use greedy or iterate through possibilities.

But n can be up to 10^6, so we need efficient solution.

Idea: sort eggs by `a_i`. Then we can try to find a subset sum in the required range.

But subset sum is NP-hard in worst case.

Alternative: we can use the fact that the constraint is small (relative to 1000).

Greedy: assign eggs to A if `a_i > 500`, to G otherwise, then check difference.

If within bounds, done. If not, try to adjust.

Actually, the difference will be from the eggs with `a_i` not equal to 500.

Another insight: since `a_i + g_i = 1000`, assigning an egg to A gives us `a_i` and to G gives us `g_i`.

The difference in total cost is `sum(a_i for A) - sum(g_i for G)` = `sum(a_i for A) - sum((1000 - a_i) for G)` = `sum(a_i for A) - sum(1000 - a_i for G)`.

Let me define: for each egg, if assigned to A, contribution to difference is `a_i`. If to G, contribution is `- (1000 - a_i)`.

So total difference = `sum over all eggs of (1 if A else - (1000 - a_i)) * value`

For egg i, if to A: +a_i, if to G: -(1000 - a_i)

So total difference = `sum(a_i for eggs to A) - sum(1000 - a_i for eggs to G)`

= `sum(a_i for eggs to A) - sum(1000 - a_i for eggs to G)`

But `sum(a_i for eggs to A) + sum(a_i for eggs to G) = sum(all a_i)`.

Let S = sum of all a_i.

Then sum(a_i for eggs to A) = x, sum(a_i for eggs to G) = S - x.

Total difference = x - sum(1000 - a_i for eggs to G) = x - [1000*(n - k) - (S - x)] where k is number assigned to A.

= x - 1000*(n - k) + S - x = S - 1000*(n - k)

This matches our earlier derivation!

So total difference = |S - 1000*(n - k)| where k is number of eggs assigned to A.

We need |S - 1000*(n - k)| ≤ 500.

So we need k such that |S - 1000*(n - k)| ≤ 500.

Let m = n - k (number of eggs assigned to G). Then |S - 1000*m| ≤ 500.

So S - 500 ≤ 1000*m ≤ S + 500.

So m is in range [ceil((S-500)/1000), floor((S+500)/1000)].

Since m must be integer, check if there exists integer m in this range such that 0 ≤ m ≤ n.

If yes, then we need to assign exactly m eggs to G.

Now, for any such m, can we find a subset of m eggs to assign to G (and the rest to A)?

This is equivalent to finding a subset of size m with sum of `a_i` such that when we assign those to G, the rest to A.

Actually, we need to find a subset of size m to assign to G.

We can do this greedily: sort eggs by `a_i` in descending order, and assign the m largest ones to G if possible.

But we need to ensure the solution exists.

We can try to use greedy assignment: for each egg, we can assign it to the child that gives the higher value.

For egg i, if `a_i > 500`, then `a_i > g_i`, so better to assign to A. If `a_i < 500`, then `g_i > a_i`, better to assign to G.

So assign based on `a_i > 500` or not.

Then calculate m = number assigned to G.

Check if |S - 1000*m| ≤ 500.

If yes, we're done. If not, try to make adjustments.

But since the constraint is tight, and if the greedy assignment doesn't work, we might need to swap.

Actually, let's implement the greedy assignment first and see.

## Implementation

```python
n = int(input())
eggs = []
for _ in range(n):
    a, g = map(int, input().split())
    eggs.append((a, g))

# Greedy assignment: assign to A if a > 500, to G otherwise
assignment = []
for a, g in eggs:
    if a > 500:
        assignment.append('A')
    else:
        assignment.append('G')

# Calculate S = sum of all a_i
S = sum(a for a, g in eggs)

# Count how many eggs are assigned to G
m = assignment.count('G')  # number of eggs to G

# Calculate difference |S - 1000*m|
diff = abs(S - 1000 * m)

if diff <= 500:
    print(''.join(assignment))
else:
    # Try to adjust by swapping
    # We need to make the difference within 500
    # Currently |S - 1000*m| = diff
    # We need to find a different m such that |S - 1000*m| <= 500 and 0 <= m <= n
    
    # Find valid m values
    min_m = (S - 500 + 999) // 1000  # ceil((S-500)/1000)
    max_m = (S + 500) // 1000       # floor((S+500)/1000)
    
    # We need to find m in [min_m, max_m] such that 0 <= m <= n
    valid_m = None
    for m_val in range(max(min_m, 0), min(max_m + 1, n + 1)):
        if m_val >= 0 and m_val <= n:
            if abs(S - 1000 * m_val) <= 500:
                valid_m = m_val
                break
    
    if valid_m is None:
        print(-1)
    else:
        # We need to have exactly m_val eggs assigned to G
        # Currently we have m eggs assigned to G
        # We need to swap some eggs between A and G
        
        # Create list of eggs with their a_i, g_i, and whether currently to G
        egg_info = []
        for i, (a, g) in enumerate(eggs):
            egg_info.append((a, g, assignment[i]))
        
        # We need to get to m_val eggs to G
        # Current count
        current_g = assignment.count('G')
        
        # If already at valid m_val, we're done
        if current_g == valid_m:
            print(''.join(assignment))
        else:
            # We need to swap (current_g - valid_m) eggs
            # We can do this by finding good candidates to swap
            
            # Let's try to create a sorted list of differences
            # When we swap an egg from A to G, we change the difference:
            # Before: we had egg with a_i assigned to A, so difference contribution: +a_i
            # After: assigned to G, contribution: -(1000 - a_i)
            # Change in difference: -(1000 - a_i) - a_i = -1000 + 2*a_i
            
            # When we swap from G to A: egg with a_i assigned to G, contribution: -(1000 - a_i)
            # After: +a_i, change: a_i - (-1000 + a_i) = 1000
            
            # Actually, total difference = S - 1000*m, so when we change m by delta, the difference changes by 1000*delta
            
            # We need to change m from current_g to valid_m, so delta = valid_m - current_g
            
            delta = valid_m - current_g
            
            # We need to change the count by delta
            # For each egg we swap:
            # If from A to G: m increases by 1, which changes the difference by 1000 (since |S - 1000*(m+1)| = |(S - 1000*m) - 1000|)
            # If from G to A: m decreases by 1, which changes the difference by -1000
            
            # But we need to make sure we don't go out of bounds
            
            # Actually, we need to have exactly valid_m eggs in G
            # We can swap any eggs
            
            # Let's create a list of eggs that can be moved
            # We want to achieve exactly valid_m in G
            
            # Instead of complex swapping, let's just reconstruct
            
            # We'll sort eggs by their "benefit" when moved
            # For eggs currently in A (a_i <= 500), moving to G: a_i increases to g_i, but we lose a_i
            # Actually, when we move an egg from A to G, we increase m by 1, and the difference changes by 1000
            
            # Actually, let's think about it differently.
            # The total difference is S - 1000*m
            # We need to make it within 500.
            # By swapping, we can change m by 1, which changes the difference by 1000.
            
            # But to change the difference by exactly 1000, we need to change m by 1.
            
            # We need to go from m to valid_m, difference change of (valid_m - m) * 1000
            
            # But we want |S - 1000*valid_m| ≤ 500, which is already guaranteed.
            
            # We just need to rearrange so that m = valid_m.
            
            # We can do this by swapping elements between A and G.
            
            # Let's collect eggs that are in A and could be moved to G (a_i > 500? no, a_i > 500 means better to keep in A)
            # Actually, eggs currently in A: for which a_i > 500
            # Eggs currently in G: for which a_i ≤ 500
            
            # When we move an egg from A to G (where a_i > 500): 
            # - m increases by 1
            # - the difference changes by 1000 (from S-1000m to S-1000(m+1))
            # But we want to reduce m by 1 when we move from G to A
            
            # We need to move |delta| eggs.
            
            # Since any swap of an egg changes m by 1, we can always achieve m = valid_m by swapping |delta| eggs.
            
            # But we need to make sure we can do it without exceeding bounds.
            
            # Actually, we can swap any egg from A to G or from G to A.
            
            # We need to create a list of eggs that can be moved (currently in A or currently in G).
            
            # For each egg, we can swap it to the other side.
            
            # So if delta > 0, we need to move delta eggs from A to G.
            # If delta < 0, we need to move |delta| eggs from G to A.
            
            # We can do this in any order.
            
            # Let's find candidates for swapping.
            
            # If delta > 0, we need to move eggs from A to G. Any egg currently in A can be moved.
            # If delta < 0, any egg currently in G can be moved.
            
            # Since we just need any valid assignment, we can move any delta eggs.
            
            # Build a list of indices to swap
            indices_to_swap = []
            
            if delta > 0:
                # Need to move from A to G
                for i, (a, g, assign) in enumerate(egg_info):
                    if assign == 'A':
                        indices_to_swap.append(i)
            else:
                # delta < 0, need to move from G to A
                for i, (a, g, assign) in enumerate(egg_info):
                    if assign == 'G':
                        indices_to_swap.append(i)
            
            # If we don't have enough indices, it's impossible
            if len(indices_to_swap) < abs(delta):
                print(-1)
            else:
                # Take the first |delta| indices
                to_swap = indices_to_swap[:abs(delta)]
                
                # Create new assignment
                new_assignment = assignment.copy()
                for i in to_swap:
                    if delta > 0:
                        # Move from A to G
                        new_assignment[i] = 'G'
                    else:
                        # Move from G to A
                        new_assignment[i] = 'A'
                
                # Verify and output
                print(''.join(new_assignment))
```

Wait, I think there's an issue. The difference constraint is `|S - 1000*m| ≤ 500`, and we're guaranteeing that for the valid_m we found. But by swapping, we're changing m by exactly 1 per swap, so we can achieve any m in the range.

But the range might not contain any valid m, so we need to check that.

Also, when we swap, we're changing m by 1, so we can reach any m between current and valid_m if they're in the range.

Actually, I think I made a mistake in the difference analysis.

Let me double-check:

Total difference = |sum_A - sum_G|

sum_A + sum_G = S

So |sum_A - (S - sum_A)| = |2*sum_A - S|

This equals |S - 2*sum_A|

But earlier I said it's |S - 1000*m| where m is number of eggs to G.

Let me recalculate carefully:

sum_G = sum of g_i for eggs assigned to G.

Let m = number of eggs assigned to G.

Then sum_G = sum of g_i for those m eggs = sum of (1000 - a_i) for those m eggs.

sum_A = S - sum_G = S - sum of (1000 - a_i) for the m eggs.

So sum_A - sum_G = (S - sum of (1000 - a_i) for m eggs) - sum of (1000 - a_i) for m eggs = S - 2 * sum of (1000 - a_i) for m eggs.

This is not S - 1000*m.

I made a mistake!

Let me recalculate the total difference.

Let S_a = total paid to A = sum of a_i for eggs assigned to A
Let S_g = total paid to G = sum of g_i for eggs assigned to G

We have S_a + S_g = sum of a_i for A + sum of g_i for G = sum of a_i for A + sum of (1000 - a_i) for G = sum of a_i for A + sum of 1000 for G - sum of a_i for G = sum of a_i for A + 1000*m - sum of a_i for G.

But sum of a_i for A + sum of a_i for G = S.

So S_a + S_g = S - sum of a_i for G + 1000*m = S - (S - sum of a_i for A) + 1000*m = sum of a_i for A + 1000*m.

This is messy.

Let me go back to the original.

For each egg i, if assigned to A: A gets a_i, G gets 0
If assigned to G: G gets g_i, A gets 0

So S_a = sum of a_i for eggs to A
S_g = sum of g_i for eggs to G = sum of (1000 - a_i) for eggs to G

The difference |S_a - S_g| = |sum_{i in A} a_i - sum_{i in G} (1000 - a_i)|

= |sum_{i in A} a_i - 1000*|G| + sum_{i in G} a_i|

= |sum_{i in A} a_i + sum_{i in G} a_i - 1000*|G|| = |S - 1000*|G||

Yes! Because sum_{i in A} a_i + sum_{i in G} a_i = sum of all a_i = S.

And |G| is the number of eggs to G.

So |S_a - S_g| = |S - 1000*|G||.

We need |S - 1000*|G|| ≤ 500.

So we need to find a number m = |G| such that |S - 1000*m| ≤ 500.

And then assign the eggs such that exactly m of them go to G.

For any such m, we can assign the eggs to achieve it, for example by greedy assignment based on a_i.

But we need to ensure that the assignment is valid.

Actually, any assignment that assigns exactly m eggs to G will satisfy the constraint.

So the only requirement is that there exists an integer m in [0, n] such that |S - 1000*m| ≤ 500.

And then we need to assign exactly m eggs to G.

We can assign in any way - for example, assign to G all eggs with a_i <= 500, and to A the others.

But this might not give exactly m.

We can use a greedy approach to assign to G a subset of size m.

To make it simple, we can assign to G the m eggs with the largest a_i, or smallest.

Actually, since the constraint only depends on m, not on which eggs, any assignment with exactly m eggs to G will work.

So we can:

1. Calculate S = sum of all a_i
2. Find if there exists m in [0, n] such that |S - 1000*m| ≤ 500
3. If not, output -1
4. If yes, then choose any m, and assign exactly m eggs to G.

But how to choose which m? We can choose any.

For example, choose m = floor((S + 500) / 1000) or whatever.

To build the assignment, we can:

- Take the first m eggs and assign to G, the rest to A.

But that might not be optimal.

Since any m in the range works, we can pick one.

But we need to ensure we can actually form such an assignment.

Since we can assign any m to G, we just need to find if such m exists.

So algorithm:

```python
n = int(input())
eggs = []
sum_a = 0
for _ in range(n):
    a, g = map(int, input().split())
    eggs.append((a, g))
    sum_a += a

# Find m such that |sum_a - 1000*m| <= 500 and 0 <= m <= n
# m must be in [ceil((sum_a - 500)/1000), floor((sum_a + 500)/1000)]

min_m = (sum_a - 500 + 999) // 1000  # ceil((sum_a-500)/1000)
max_m = (sum_a + 500) // 1000        # floor((sum_a+500)/1000)

# Check if there's any m in [min_m, max_m] within [0, n]
valid_m = None
for m in range(max(min_m, 0), min(max_m + 1, n + 1)):
    if abs(sum_a - 1000 * m) <= 500:
        valid_m = m
        break

if valid_m is None:
    print(-1)
else:
    # We need to assign exactly valid_m eggs to G
    # We can assign any valid_m eggs to G, the rest to A.
    # For simplicity, assign to G the first valid_m eggs.
    result = []
    for i in range(n):
        if i < valid_m:
            result.append('G')
        else:
            result.append('A')
    print(''.join(result))
```

Let me test with the examples.

Example 1:
n=2, eggs: (1,999), (999,1)
sum_a = 1 + 999 = 1000
We need |1000 - 1000*m| <= 500
For m=1: |1000-1000| = 0 <= 500, good.
So valid_m = 1.
Assign first 1 to G, second to A: "GA" -> matches expected.

Example 2:

|1200 - 1000*m| <= 500
For m=1: |1200-1000| = 200 <= 500
For m=2: |1200-2000| = 800 > 500
For m=0: |1200| = 1200 > 500
For m=3: |1200-3000| = 1800 > 500
So m=1 is valid.
Assign first 1 to G, next 2 to A: "GA" "A" -> "GAA"
But expected is "AGA", which has 2 eggs to G.

For m=2: |1200-2000| = 800 > 500, not valid.
For m=1: valid.

But "AGA" has 2 eggs to G, m=2, which is not valid.

Let me check "AGA":
- Egg1: A gets 400, G gets 0
- Egg2: G gets 600, A gets 0
- Egg3: A gets 400, G gets 0
- S_a = 400 + 400 = 800
- S_g = 600
- |800 - 600| = 200 <= 500. Oh! This is valid.

What is m here? m = number of eggs to G = 1 (only egg2).

For "AGA", only egg2 is to G, so m=1.

In "AGA", the first egg to A, second to G, third to A.

So m=1.

In my assignment, I assign first 1 to G, so "GAA" or "GA" then "A" - for first 3, "GAA".

In "GAA": 
- Egg1: G gets 600
- Egg2: A gets 400
- Egg3: A gets 400
- S_a = 400 + 400 = 800
- S_g = 600
- |800-600|=200 <= 500. Valid.

Both "GAA" and "AGA" have m=1.

The problem says "if there are several solutions, print any".

So my solution should work.

In example 2, any assignment with exactly 1 egg to G will work.

So in my code, I'll assign the first valid_m eggs to G.

The valid_m is the first m in [min_m, max_m] that satisfies the condition.


This should work. Let me verify the ceil and floor.

For ceil(x/1000): if x is negative, need to be careful.
